## Preface.

I could all the less excuse myself from the occasion of these letters, since a friend—who, however, is not to be sought in Zurich <note>Original: Zürch</note> this time—most earnestly requested my opinion regarding this Jesuit Order <note>Original: Jesusorden; the Society of Jesus</note>, and many phenomena connected with it. The older I have grown in my career as a public academic teacher of what is called theology: the more earnestly I have always strived to advance the common good by making Christian moral religion more accessible, and to gradually undermine the source of the domination over Christians through human inventions and their artful promotions. With all seriousness, I have [endeavored to separate] church theology <term>Kirchentheologie: the formal, dogmatic study of religion as defined by institutional church authorities</term> and church language from the moral, free [religion] belonging to every thinking Christian—

<sig>a 2</sig>

<meta>catchword: Relig</meta>

<summary>In this preface, the author explains that he is writing these letters at the request of a friend to address the influence of the Jesuit Order. He describes his lifelong academic mission to simplify Christianity into a moral, personal faith free from institutional manipulation.</summary>
<keywords>Jesuit Order, Society of Jesus, Zurich, theology, Christian morality, church authority, religious reform, academic teaching</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s explanation of the distinction between institutional church theology and a personal, moral religion. It picks up from the previous page’s discussion of separating "church theology" from "moral, free religion."</meta>

Religion, I have sought to distinguish; [I have strived] to make the former known as that which is accidental <note>In this context, "accidental" (zufällig) refers to things that are non-essential or contingent, such as specific rituals or local customs, rather than the core truths of faith.</note> and changeable, always dependent on a particular society, through the undeniable assistance of history. Conversely, my goal is to truly represent and recommend this moral, personal religion of Christians in that same freedom and universality which finds free entry into the hearts of men now, just as it did among those who first recognized this more perfect universal religion and preferred it over the Judaism and Paganism of that time.

This aligns with the foremost fundamental article of the Christian religion, according to which we believe and sing that the infinite Spirit of God unites people from all over the world, from all nations, tongues, and languages, in unity of faith, in one moral ground, and in one moral Christ. This is achieved without any requirement for unity of language, of formulas, of measures, or of methods of teaching; in short, without a worldly kingdom, a political government, or the domination of all people through...

<summary>The author argues for a "moral religion" that is universal and internal, distinguishing it from institutional "church theology" which is shaped by historical accidents. This divine, holy principle, standing in opposition to the common Judaism and Paganism <note>The author uses "Paganism" (Heidentum) to refer to the non-Abrahamic, polytheistic religions of the Roman world.</note> of the time, is the universal, the first, and the greatest aspect of the Christian religion. This divine and holy principle, set against the common Judaism and Paganism of that era, is the universal essence, the first and greatest part of the Christian religion. Small churches together formed a great collective church, or the **Catholic Church**; and so people forgot, neglected, and eventually even hindered the **true moral, spiritual religion**, through which all who know and love it, in East and West, are already united of their own accord; despite any degree of inequality and diversity in all external, inherently non-moral, circumstances. VII
A 4

is, without offending one another. But it was precisely that low, self-serving spirit of the corrupted Jewish and pagan religions that very soon passed over to the teachers and leaders of the new, or Christian, religion.

They were meant to promote moral knowledge <note>original: *moralische Kentnisse*; in this context, it refers to practical wisdom and ethical living as the core of faith.</note> ever more and without ceasing, for the common good of an increasing number of people; they were meant to illuminate ever more the infinite glory and self-sufficiency <note>original: *Algenugsamkeit*; a theological term implying that God is complete in Himself and the source of all satisfaction.</note> of God for the salvation of all, as a continuing blessing for mankind. Instead, they created laws and regulations that could only ever be applied toward external unification—and thus toward the mastery and oppression of people—as convenient means to an end. The Church became a state, a monarchy.

This entirely false unity of external religion, which is morally impossible and completely denies the majesty of God, is well known to all thinking Christians; only a few wise rulers have occasionally [resisted] this <note>The sentence ends mid-thought, continuing on the next page; the catchword "fals" suggests the next word is "falsche" (false).</note>

VIII

I do not wish to dwell further on this here; all those centuries in which people—regardless of how good, moral, and virtuous they were—were called heretics <note>original: *Kezer*; historically, individuals whose beliefs or practices deviated from official Church dogma.</note> and were physically tormented or killed to maintain the Church in all its usurped honor: these centuries speak for themselves. I do not know if I may say that Protestants, too, were backed into a corner all too soon by the political accusations of the old Church <note>The Roman Catholic Church.</note>—accusations concerning the ever-increasing divisions and differences among Protestant teachers. Gradually, they also began to insist too much on external unity and the immutability of the doctrinal order, formulas of faith, and the established language of the church. They did not only bind their teachers to these rules—which the community <note>original: *Gesellschaft*; here referring to the religious society or denomination.</note> was free to do—but they even went so far as to make the salvation <note>original: *Seligkeit*; refers to eternal happiness or the state of being saved.</note> of their church members dependent from then on upon a uniform description of Christian ideas and truths. They began to call this ecclesiastical standard <note>original: *Kirchliche Maas*</note> the only soul-saving doctrine and religion. I may certainly say that this could lead to an incorrect and harmful mixing of external religion with <term>moral religion</term> <note>In the context of the Enlightenment, "moral religion" refers to an internal, ethical faith focused on virtue and character, as opposed to "statutory" or "external" religion focused on rituals and specific dogmas.</note>. XII

**Language of Religion, Summary of Religion, and Religious Order.** As early as the year 1698, the **Philadelphian Society** <note>The Philadelphian Society was a 17th-century religious movement, primarily in England, that emphasized internal spiritual rebirth and universal brotherhood over external church structures.</note> had published: it is no longer a secret that the clergy of the **French Church**—even the most eminent among them, in terms of learning and intellect as well as godliness—are imbued and saturated with the very same principles upon which this Society is founded; — — — it is beginning to become widely known that this spiritual zeal and devotion is gaining the upper hand not only among the clergy and members of religious orders, but also among the laity and those who have their daily conduct and interactions both in the world and at court. That very **Cardinal** <note>Likely a reference to Cardinal Richelieu (1585–1642), who is credited with establishing French "Hoheit" (supremacy) in Europe. XIII

XIV

I could write out many more such fitting passages if I did not wish to be brief. Meanwhile, I cannot overlook the fact that precisely this concept of a Society that is indeed cohesive—and thus truly connected—likewise seems to me a means that is, in itself, more of a hindrance than a help to infinite **moral religion** <term>moral religion: *moralische Religion*, an Enlightenment-era ideal where the core of faith is ethical conduct and reason, rather than external rituals or dogmatic structures</term>: and sooner or later it will or must lead to a **Philadelphian monarchy** <note>The author suggests that even a group based on "brotherly love" (Philadelphia) could become a centralized, sovereign power if it becomes too organized.</note>, even if an old-style papacy and political church-state do not result from it. The admirers of this Society cling far too much to turns of phrase, to mystical images borrowed from the **Bible**; the constant continuation of which is not always as necessary as it once was for connoisseurs and friends of moral religion. They therefore also have principles that are too narrow, and they do not wish to, and cannot, [emerge] from the mixture of physical effects...

XV

...people who might scent things that are contrary to church power? Right from the beginning, the emerging Church protected itself everywhere with the word **apostolic**; everything was to be derived immediately from the **adepts** <note>original: *Adepten*. While this term is often associated with alchemy to mean an "initiate," the author uses it here to refer to those claiming to be the exclusive successors or "insiders" of the apostles' secrets.</note>; and the Christians—should and must now unconditionally obey the Church. 

But these assumptions are certainly no part of the Christian religion: they are very cleverly placed conclusions and inferences; however, the logic of the Church is just as narrow and biased as when the **rabbis** <note>Jewish religious leaders and scholars.</note> would cite a **verse** <term>posuk|From the Hebrew *pasuk*; a verse or sentence of scripture used as proof in a theological argument.</term>. We Protestants have never held the principle that everything attributed to the apostles, or that can be attributed to them, is a general, indispensable **article of faith** for all good Christians; just as little as the apostles themselves or their writings were, or could become, articles of faith. 

At the very least, I cannot help but draw the attention of our contemporaries more and more to that so ambiguous and dangerous phrase: **old pure doctrine**; the old, incipient, original religion of the Christians... <note>The text cuts off here, continuing the word "Christians" (Christen) on the following page.</note>

<meta>catchword: -tians (sten)</meta>

<summary>The author argues that the early Church used the label "apostolic" as a tool for control rather than a core religious requirement. He warns fellow Protestants against the dangerous nostalgia for an "old pure doctrine," asserting that neither the apostles nor their writings should be treated as rigid articles of faith.</summary>

## Ninth Letter.
There have always been alternating, periodic methods of teaching the Christian religion. The authors of the Pastoral Letter <note>The "Pastoral Letter" (Hirtenbrief) refers to a 1782 document issued by the leaders of the Rosicrucian Order, which sought to enforce a specific, mystical orthodoxy.</note> are not satisfied with this. All restrictions on private religion stem from external motives, 141. Not all books of the Bible belong forever to the foundation of the Christian religion, 143. Skill in teaching <note>original: "Lehrgeschicklichkeit"; the pedagogical talent or method used to convey religious truths.</note> was always free, 144.

So, in your region as well, people are gradually becoming aware of an unknown society that counts itself among the **Freemasons**, and claims to belong to the **old system**! <note>The "old system" likely refers to the "Strict Observance," a branch of Freemasonry that claimed descent from the Knights Templar, or the specific Rosicrucian system mentioned in the preface.</note> Certainly, this occurrence in **Winterthur** <note>A city in Switzerland; the mention suggests the spread of these secret societies into Swiss territories.</note> is a very striking phenomenon! ...enjoy protection; on the other hand, the state should not grant such sudden, completely unknown connections and societies an unrestricted <term>scrutiny</term> <note>original: "Muſterung"; while it can mean a military inspection, here it refers to a systematic evaluation or "muster" of citizens' lives and loyalties by a private group.</note> over its very diverse subjects. *fanaticism*, which is instilled in deceived, well-meaning Christians. They were always building toward a clerical monarchy; and a large portion of hidden *Jesuits* <note>During the late 18th century, Enlightenment thinkers often feared that the Jesuit Order—suppressed by the Pope in 1773—was continuing to operate in secret to undermine Protestant states.</note> is now building among *Protestants*. — So much has the old system of the noble, worthy, *Freemasons*, and those *Rosicrucians* <note>Refers to the "Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross," a conservative, mystical, and alchemical branch of Masonry that was very influential in the Prussian court at this time.</note> announcing themselves in Germany <note>original: "Teutschland"</note>, changed among certain real or alleged *descendants*!

Without further ado, I come to the three questions you have presented to me regarding the so-called *Pastoral Letter* <note>original: "Hirtenbrief"; this refers to the "Pastoral Letter to the True and Genuine Freemasons of the Old System," a famous and controversial document published in 1785.</note> to the true and genuine *Freemasons* of the Old System. Let those who consume their existence in their own laziness and idleness think of the "Golden Age."

Now I continue once more to collect descriptions of those who deemed it useful to issue this Pastoral Letter <term>Hirtenbrief: Traditionally a letter from a Bishop to his flock, here used as the title of the secret society's manifesto.</term>. Following pages 7, 8, and 9, this mixture ——— follows: "Yes, that is who we are; (**Stewards** <term>Haushalter: A term for administrators of divine mysteries, likely referencing 1 Corinthians 4:1.</term> of the despised Jesus;) this you shall find united in us, your spiritual fathers..."

In the Age of Enlightenment, many secret societies were criticized for demanding that members suppress their critical thinking.</note>, (as is spoken here on page 29 so fatherly, or rather, in the manner of the old system of the **Popes** <note>original: "Päbste"</note>;) 

which deviations these "Fathers" claim will cause a God—who is indeed long-suffering, but also just as jealous of His honor—to provide for everything regarding these apostate or disobedient children! ### Postscript.

I cannot assume that my additions to the German translation of the *Apology of the Rosicrucians* <note>referring to a defense of the Rosicrucian order, likely the *Apologia flosculis Hieroglyphicis* or a similar work</note> are also known in your region; therefore, I wish to briefly summarize this matter here, especially since I must add something regarding the **Elector of the Palatinate** <note>The ruler of the Electoral Palatinate, a territory of the Holy Roman Empire; Frederick IV or V was prominent during this era's alchemical boom.</note>. In the year 1601, on the 1st of January, *Nicolas Barnaud* <note>Nicolas Barnaud (c. 1538–1604), a French physician and alchemist who promoted "hermetic" ideas across Europe.</note> wrote to all *French philosophers* <note>original: "Philosophos... gallos"</note> (hermetic devotees), (or so he claimed) that he was handing this essay over to them on the condition that they might work successfully for the honor of God, the benefit of Christ’s Church, and the service of the most Christian King, *Henry the Great* <note>Henry IV of France.</note>. <meta>This page provides the specific citations from Jakob Böhme that the author alluded to on the previous page. The author continues his critique of the "Jesuits" (the Order's leaders) by providing the reader with the source text for the "center of the sun" concept.</meta>

So that looking it up is not too difficult for you, I will write it out here for your sake. Jakob Böhme, *Great Mystery* <note>original Latin: "mysterium magnum"; Böhme’s 1623 commentary on Genesis</note>, Chapter 12, starting from paragraph 3:

> “The planetary wheel emerged from the **point of the sun** <note>original: "puncto solis"</note>. For there was the royal seat of the **Hierarchy** <term>Hierarchie; the organized ranks of spiritual or angelic beings</term>, of which the entire circle—between the stars in the interior and the exterior—is a member or **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note>. Paragraph 4: Because the Prince of the Hierarchy [Lucifer] fell and raised himself into the **center** <note>original: "Centrum"</note> of eternal night, he was cast into darkness; and through His movement, God created another prince for him out of this place, but without divine understanding, to be a ruler of the **essences** <term>Essenzen; the fundamental spiritual qualities or powers that make up material reality</term>—that is the sun. Paragraph 5: From this place, in the movement of God, the seven properties of nature emerged; understand these as the planets, which rule the **essential** being in both good and evil; in which **Lucifer** sat, and from which he was cast out.”

## Fifth Letter.

I am indeed entirely of the opinion that I would not find it necessary at all to first represent the **Luciferiad** <term>Luciferiade; a narrative concerning the fall of Lucifer and the origin of evil, common in mystical cosmogonies</term> communicated in the **pastoral letter** <term>Hirtenbrief; a formal circular letter issued by the leaders of a secret order</term> as such: namely, that it has been borrowed completely and utterly, in all its material, directly from **Böhme’s** <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624), a German Lutheran mystic whose works on the nature of God and evil were highly influential</note> writings; even though **Weigel** <note>Valentin Weigel (1533–1588), a German theologian and predecessor to Böhme in the mystical tradition</note> and others have indeed already worked on this theme. Only these **Jesuits** <note>The Society of Jesus; the author is accusing a secret society—likely the Gold- and Rosicrucians—of being a front for a Catholic/Jesuit agenda to reclaim influence in Protestant lands</note> are reintroducing the old, dark **papal rule** <term>Pabstregierung; the ecclesiastical authority of the Pope</term> into all German provinces, and they wish to take the readers captive. to your friends. I must now add just one more remark and refutation regarding that loquacious Pastoral Letter <note>original: "Hirtenbrief"; these were official circular letters sent by the leaders of the Order of the Gold and Rosy Cross to their members to dictate doctrine.</note>.

---

On p. 64, just before the mythology of Lucifer, it is stated: “the inhabitants of the inner spiritual world, whom the Scriptures call flaming fires <note>original: "Feuerflammen"; a reference to Psalm 104:4</note> (Ps. 104, Heb. 1:7), were the firstlings of all creatures. They are, in the most perfect sense of the word, true flaming fires; a composition of fire and light.” — This is intended to subtly prove that the system is directly biblical. I will not linger on this point for long; anyone proficient in the original languages <note>The author likely refers to Hebrew and Greek, implying that a proper study of the original biblical texts refutes the Order's mystical interpretations.</note> knows that the meaning of the Psalm is entirely different. It does not speak of spiritual substances or angels; it does not say that they consist of fire and light. The author is speaking of the fiery phenomena and lightning bolts familiar to us, which God uses as servants and messengers to us inhabitants of the earth; just as storm winds on this earth of ours certainly bring about the great changes that are necessary on the earth. It is, therefore, simply not true that the inhabitants of the inner spiritual world whom the Pastoral Letter establishes are being described here.

than the first <note>Referring to the previous question of "who" wrote the letter</note>; for I am just as unable to name specific individuals, in this city or that, as dissident members of the **Jesuit Order** <note>original: "Jesusorden"</note> of the old system, that I even strongly doubt whether this Pastoral Letter has a historical or local *personal target* <note>original Latin: "obiectum personale"</note>. I am reminded of so many letters that were distributed through various printings in the previous century, in Latin and German, under the name of the **Rosicrucians** <note>A legendary secret society of sages and alchemists whose 17th-century manifestos caused a sensation in Europe</note>, which were truly meant to be delivered to that historical man, *Nobody* <note>original: "Utis," the Greek word for "Nobody" used by Odysseus to trick the Cyclops</note>, on the moon. Among these, two letters in particular were printed quite frequently: a letter from the Brothers of the Rosy Cross concerning the invisible magical mountain and the treasure guarded within it; *Everyone is a leader by nature, etc.* <note>original Latin: "Vnus quisque natura dux esse &c."</note> Everyone naturally desires to be a ruler, etc., etc. Or the *Response* <note>original Latin: "Respons"</note> from the **F.R.C.** <note>Fraternitas Rosae Crucis, or the Brotherhood of the Rosy Cross</note> given to several of their clients. The first: "Everyone naturally desires great—treasures, etc." The other *Response*: "I enter into the seven circles of heaven, etc." I myself have held quite a few editions of these in my hands; I even still possess five or six copies. And this *Response* is addressed to "N. N." <note>A placeholder for an anonymous name, from the Latin "nomen nescio" (I do not know the name)</note>. Thus, I judge this Pastoral Letter in the same way; it is addressed to no single individuals who might be found at a specific time or place through historical locality—

Neither the perfect nor those brothers who are still on the (right) path are our focus; but rather only new, still inexperienced brothers who are simultaneously hungering for truth and **Enlightenment** <note>original: "Aufklärung"</note> (**Enlightenment** is supposed to be in this compilation!)—these are our subject, our target point, our flock to whom we speak! # Seventh Letter.

I am really not counting according to the seven source- or nature-spirits <note>A reference to the *Quellgeister* or seven properties of nature described by the mystic Jacob Böhme, which form the basis of his cosmology.</note>. I still have to answer your third question: what does this **Pastoral Letter** actually teach? However, I believe that I have already contributed a great deal toward answering this question in the previous letters; for I could not alter the pervasive dogmatic tone of the **Pastoral Letter**. Therefore, be satisfied if I strive from now on to draw closer to the end of this—to me, certainly bothersome—scribbling, without, however, being too unmindful of your expectations. I am skipping everything concerning **Lucifer**, the **celestial Adam** <term>Celestial Adam: In esoteric thought, the "Adam Kadmon" or original spiritual human before the fall into material form.</term>, <term>etc.</term>, and will only note again that the **Pastoral Letter** consequently copies the theory of **Lucifer** purely according to **Jacob Böhme** <term>Jacob Böhme: (1575–1624), a German Lutheran mystic whose complex spiritual system was a primary source for 18th-century Rosicrucianism.</term>, and has entirely abandoned the older theory, according to which **Lucifer** refused to serve **Adam**; a theory which a great lover and follower of the old **Rosicrucians** <term>Rosicrucians: A secret society claiming ancient wisdom, which saw a major revival in Germany during the 1760s–80s as the "Gold and Rosy Cross."</term> still retained. I mean the so-called **Eugenius Philalethes** <note>The pseudonym of Thomas Vaughan (1621–1666), a Welsh alchemist and clergyman whose works were central to the hermetic tradition.</note>, in his *Anthroposophia Theomagica*, page 44 of the German translation from the year 1704, octavo. In the writing *Lumen de Lumine* [Light from Light], he had a large portion of the well-known Response of the **Rosicrucians** printed (*everyone desires to be a leader by nature* <note>original: "vnus quisque natura dux efse defiderat"</note>). This

## Eighth Letter.

I am writing you yet another letter; certainly not for the sake of the holy number eight <note>The number eight often symbolizes new beginnings or spiritual rebirth in mystical numerology.</note>; but because I consider it beneficial for the general good to prevent certain misunderstandings in particular. If I wanted to demonstrate in such detail that the entire foundation, the whole content, has long been known among us—namely from **Jacob Böhme** <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a German Christian mystic whose writings on the "divine essence" heavily influenced later secret societies.</note>, **Gichtel** <note>Johann Georg Gichtel (1638–1710), a prominent follower of Böhme and leader of the "Angelic Brothers."</note>, **Pordage** <note>John Pordage (1607–1681), an English physician and mystic who spread Behmenist ideas.</note>, **Samuel Richter**, or **Sincerus Renatus** <note>Sincerus Renatus was the pseudonym of Samuel Richter, an influential author whose 1710 work helped define the Rosicrucian revival.</note> etc. (not to mention even older dissidents who were directly called **Brothers of the Free Spirit** <note>original: *fratres liberi spiritus*. A lay Christian movement of the 13th and 14th centuries that emphasized the "inner light" and was often accused of heresy.</note> and practiced **alchemy** <term>alchemy: (German: *Alchymie*) The ancient tradition of transforming base metals into gold, used here as a metaphor for spiritual purification.</term> in abundance); [showing that it] could have been compiled even if this **letter of the Order** <note>German: *Ordensbrief*. A formal instructional document or circular sent to members of a secret society.</note> had remained entirely unprinted: then I did not intend to simultaneously disparage Jacob Böhme, Gichtel, Pordage, etc.; rather, I wanted to publicly prove that these pathetic **superiors of the Order** <term>superiors of the Order: (German: *Ordensobern*) The high-ranking leaders of a secret society, often claimed to possess secret, divine authority.</term> [acted] very dishonestly...

I am convinced that all true Freemasons <term>Freimäurer; a fraternal organization that, in the 18th century, was often associated with Enlightenment ideals of reason and secularism, though it also faced accusations of secret political or religious agendas.</term> are true, eternal opponents of all Popery <note>original: "Pabsttum." (Might we not say that lovers of light have to fight against the so-proud Lucifer! <note>Lucifer literally means "Light-bringer." The author is making a play on words: the "lovers of light" (Enlightened thinkers) are fighting a "Light-bringer" who is actually the prince of pride and darkness.</note>) Therefore, one also seeks to draw noble ranks into such intentional associations! That is why false brothers allow themselves to be given the tonsure <note>The practice of shaving the top of the head to indicate clerical status in the Catholic Church.</note> in secret darkness! That is why they are beginning to make entirely new glosses <note>Explanatory notes or commentaries.</note> upon the Mass; that is why the pastoral letter <term>Hirtenbrief; an official letter from a bishop to the clergy or laity.</term> recommends the "restoration of all things"—meaning the restoration of the Protestants as well, and of that first, so beautifully tinctured (or rather, fictitious) apostolic church. I will leave it to you to decide whether I speak too suspiciously or hypochondriacally! The tincture of the body and blood of Christ in the Lord’s Supper <note>Communion or the Eucharist.</note> (the transformation of the bread)—and that passage on page 121; do read it with reflection.

The authors of the *Pastoral Letter* looked around at their potential audience. They are themselves truly already determined to oppose so-called (human) erudition, because through it, the ancient <term>principle of authority <note>original: *principium auctoritatis*</note></term> is increasingly weakened. 

It was precisely upon this prestige and authority that the entire former papal church constitution rested. Free inquiry—a spirit of research, one might say—is spreading more and more, even among Roman Catholic princes and prelates. 

— The current Emperor <note>Emperor Joseph II (reigned 1765–1790), known for "Josephinism," a series of Enlightenment reforms that restricted the power of the Catholic Church and promoted religious tolerance.</note> acts based on insights and principles, as has always been the honor and duty of all great, worthy sovereign rulers. Notwithstanding that true, perfect religion requires absolutely no external assistance for its existence and growth—because everything external is limited and isolated by time and place, occurring within individual human beings who are and remain entirely different from one another, and who thus can only take their own local, individual share of true and pure religion—I am nevertheless not at all opposed to smaller societies seeking out and maintaining their proximity to one another, so to speak, as soon as they actually find themselves in genuine agreement. 

But is it possible that individual humans, or societies—who always find themselves united by time and place and equally separated from others—do not realize that they can no more enlarge their individual moral **standpoint** <term>Standplaz: A philosophical term referring to one's subjective point of view or moral position, which is limited by personal experience and context.</term>, make it universal and infinite, and offer it directly to all other moral beings, than they can transplant their physical existence into all times and all regions, or place other humans into their own time and their own location?

<meta>This page continues the author's critique of religious institutionalization. The previous page ended by noting that the author had never been convinced that the "man-made order" of the early church was intended to be a divine, eternal mandate, even though modern Protestant and Catholic churches treat it as such.</meta>

In my view, all the writings of the New Testament were written for the first teachers of that time, so that they all might have this or that guide <note>original: "Leitfaden"</note> to spread the new **moral religion** <term>Moral Religion: A concept, popular during the Enlightenment, defining faith through ethical conduct and virtue rather than through rituals or specific dogmas.</term>, against dead **Judaism**, **Paganism**, or against the personal immoral habits and fruitless dispositions of those people. Therefore, these books do not all belong—one as well as the other—to the sources for the future doctrine of the subsequent **Christian religion**. 

They also do not exclusively contain all the concepts and all the truths which all Christians, in all future times and especially in their entirely different circumstances, would have to utilize. And just as little are the customs or regulations of that time simply eternal mandates and orders for all Christians; unless very unpracticed readers cling to the literal letter of the text. Thus, it may have been edifying for many who still continue the practice of **foot-washing** <note>Foot-washing: A ritual based on Jesus washing his disciples' feet (John 13); the author suggests that while it was a meaningful historical gesture, it need not be an eternal requirement.</note> even now. 

But then comes the so-called **Church**, and establishes that all books of the Old and New Testaments must be and remain a single whole, in order to derive the Christian religion from them. The teachers and **bishops** have the privilege to explain these books to Christians, once and for all, and to determine their private application. This **church-religion** <term>Church-religion (Kirchenreligion): Institutionalized faith governed by hierarchy and established dogma, which the author contrasts with a more personal, rational "moral religion."</term> is thus once and for all

<summary>The author argues that New Testament writings were practical guides for early missionaries rather than eternal laws, critiquing the Church for institutionalizing these historical texts into a rigid, mandatory "church-religion."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s critique of the Protestant clerical establishment (the "Lehrstand") mentioned at the end of page 148. <page-num>149</page-num>

of the teaching profession <term>original: "Lehrstand"; the professional body of clergy and theologians</term>, has been able to be restricted or abolished. I do not wish to cite individual examples; just think of the sluggish history of **exorcism** <note>original: "Exorcismus"; in early Lutheran baptismal rites, a minor exorcism was often performed to renounce the devil. Its removal was a point of intense conservative resistance.</note> at baptism; of the zealous defense of the **Formula of Concord** <note>original: "formula concordiae"; the 1577 document that solidified Lutheran orthodoxy but was seen by critics as stifling intellectual growth.</note>, (which is, after all, a quite wretched compilation from the **Swabian Confession** <note>original: "confessione Sueuica"; a regional statement of faith that served as a source for later Lutheran dogmatics.</note>); of the great uniformity of theological compendiums—the teachers from Helmstedt <note>The University of Helmstedt was famous for "Syncretism," a more moderate and ecumenical approach to theology that was often attacked by hardline Lutherans.</note> excepted; of the history of the enterprising Spener <note>Philipp Jakob Spener (1635–1705), the founder of Pietism, who moved away from rigid dogma toward personal "piety of the heart."</note> and his successors; of the history of the Herrnhuters <note>The Moravian Brethren, a communal pietist group that faced significant opposition from the established church.</note>, even thirty years ago, and so forth. Everywhere you find a presupposed immutability of the theology used until now, even though the authors of these theological compendiums could hardly possess divine infallibility.

I am not at all surprised that many scholars of the Roman Church, and especially the Jesuits and their followers, were so happy to raise their voices regarding the lack of uniformity among some academic Protestant teachers. They could always count on the fact that many Protestants were lying in wait for one another, and would apply against each other that same old political principle of the monarchical church: that no newer—that is, modern, current—knowledge was to be tolerated, for fear that one might deviate from the true, pure doctrine!

<meta>This page continues the critique of religious institutionalization, specifically how the "teaching profession" (clergy) solidified their power by turning flexible spiritual truths into rigid dogmas. It follows the discussion on page 149 regarding the resistance to reform within the Lutheran church.</meta>

<page-num>150</page-num>

So artfully did they confuse the conscientious respect for practical Christian truths with all the specific, individual, mandated descriptions of them prescribed by the church; these latter often hindered the constant flow <note>original: "Fluxus"; refers to the natural change or fluidity of human thought.</note> of ideas and that harmless diversity for no other reason than so the church could rule over Christians. Very soon, an imaginary belief in the physical effect of holy formulas and ceremonies crept in; and from then on, it was certainly a settled matter: whoever does not keep the language of the church pure and unchanged must also go without its power and effect for salvation <note>original: "Seligkeit"; a state of eternal happiness or blessedness.</note>. Now, the immutability and indispensability of the concepts and turns of phrase established by the church were decided once and for all. Whoever does not wish to belong to the Pope, or to the church, cannot be saved; they are a scorner or blasphemer of the holy religion. — — — Now nothing but hatred and resentment was sown, the constant growth of which ensured the continued preservation of this very church-religion for centuries; until wise and determined rulers once again eased the servitude of Christians.

The authors of the <term>Pastoral Letter</term> <note>original: "Hirtenbrief"; likely a reference to the 1788 Religious Edict issued by Johann Christoph von Wöllner in Prussia, which sought to curb the Enlightenment's influence on the clergy.</note> support this very same old system of the monarchical church; they teach a physical, essential connection...

<meta>catchword: mens</meta>

<summary>This page describes how church authorities maintained control by mandating specific theological language and attributing mystical power to rituals, arguing that this created a system of "servitude" only broken by enlightened rulers.</summary>
<keywords>ecclesiastical authority, dogma, salvation, religious servitude, Pastoral Letter, church history, Enlightenment, religious language</keywords>

--- Page 205 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous page's discussion of the "Pastoral Letter," completing the word "connection" (Zusammenhang) from the previous catchword. It continues to critique the Jesuit Order's methods of influence and their alchemical metaphors for spiritual control.</meta>

<page-num>151</page-num>

...connection of **rebirth** with the **Jesus-tincture** <note>original: "Jesustinktur"; an alchemical term adapted by theosophists to describe a spiritual substance or "medicine" that transforms the soul.</note>; and this is only in the hands of the **Jesuit Order**. The **Superiors** alone possess the gift of **projection** <note>original: "Projektion"; in alchemy, the final stage of transforming base metal into gold; here used metaphorically for the transformation of the soul.</note>; whoever does not allow themselves to be **tinctured** <note>original: "tingiren"; to be infused with a spiritual quality.</note> by them is—**lost**. **Priestly ordination** is absolutely necessary in the **Roman Church** for the power and effect of the **Sacraments**. All **Catholics** should remember the **preface**: that one must love the **truth** under every garment; they should therefore, for the time being, not be so strict with this (**periodic**) **economy** <note>original: "Haushaltung"; refers to the theological concept of "economy of truth," or the strategic management of information to achieve a religious goal.</note>, by which one ultimately wishes to bring the **Protestants** back to the **flock**, under the supreme shepherd, the **Pope**. **Protestants**, however, would not see the (proper) **truth** under this garment, and thus, with good intentions, would ascribe a very edifying **meaning** to it, and certainly trust the **Jesuit Order** with more goodness than they otherwise would the **Jesuits**. Even the very public **appeal**, claiming they were writing to the **flock** entrusted to them by Jesus, is quite naturally and easily connected to the old **papal reservation**. **Mary** is and remains the **mother of grace and mercy** <note>original: "mater gratiae und misericordiae"</note>, as before. —

Do you not also think, dear friend, it is time for honest, patriotic **Protestants** to become wise as serpents! If some **mon-** <note>Catchword "mon-" likely refers to "Monarchs" or "Monks" in the following page.</note>

<summary>This page critiques the Jesuit Order for using alchemical metaphors and "theological economy" to deceptively lure Protestants back into the Catholic fold under the Pope's authority. The author warns Protestants to be "wise as serpents" against these mystical claims of spiritual transformation.</summary>
<keywords>Jesuits, Jesus-tincture, Alchemy, Papacy, Protestants, Counter-Reformation, Religious Deception, Mary</keywords>

--- Page 206 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's critique of the Jesuit "Pastoral Letter." It completes the word "Monarchs" (Monarchen) from the previous page's catchword and explores the conflict between ecclesiastical authority and Enlightenment-era academic freedom.</meta>

<page-num>152</page-num>

[mo]narchs first love and accept this **tincture theory** <note>original: "Tinkturtheorie"; the author refers to the spiritual-alchemical doctrine used by the Jesuits to claim exclusive power over the "transformation" of the soul.</note>, do you think that increasingly sharper **pastoral letters** <note>original: "Hirtenbriefe"; official circular letters sent by a bishop or church authority to their "flock."</note> will not follow? Who is obstructed by **university erudition**, the free application of the **intellect**? Precisely the **ecclesiastical monarchy**; it was against this very thing that **universities** were formerly established by wise and prudent **rulers**. You will find the consequences of unhindered **knowledge** in some states to be very great, regarding the former vast power of the **Church** and **clergy** <note>original: "Klerisey"</note>. Ask all industrious **subjects**, all those whom one called **laity**, whether they are dissatisfied, whether they complain about the **restriction** of the **Church** through wise **laws**? You will find few or no disgruntled people. Who is it, then, who has cause to be dissatisfied with the steady, free growth of all **knowledge**? Answer that yourself.

Furthermore, it may also be that the authors of the **pastoral letter** also primarily had in view the numerous **Protestants**, to whom **Jacob Böhme’s** writings are very dear (rightly so, as I have already admitted <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a German mystic whose complex, often alchemical language was popular among spiritualists of various denominations.</note>). It may be that these **masters of the Order** hoped, here and there, to spread this **devout chemical taste**, this **theosophical alchemy**, quite extensively,

<summary>The author argues that the Church opposes university education because it threatens their "monarchy" and praises secular laws that have limited clerical power. He suggests the Jesuits are using the popular "theosophical alchemy" of Jacob Böhme to covertly influence Protestants.</summary>

<keywords>ecclesiastical monarchy, university erudition, Enlightenment, clergy vs laity, Jacob Böhme, theosophical alchemy, Jesuit Order, religious politics</keywords>

--- Page 207 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's critique of the Jesuit "Pastoral Letter." The text completes the phrase "chemical prophets" from the previous page, where the author suggested the authors of the letter were targeting those who followed the mystical alchemical traditions of Jacob Böhme.</meta>

<page-num>153</page-num>
<sig>K 5</sig>

[chemical pro]phets, and wanted to more certainly deflect the detrimental attention from themselves, which had already put many contemporaries into some embarrassment—those who counted themselves among the **Freemasons** <note>Freemasonry in the 18th century often intersected with various mystical and alchemical societies, sometimes drawing suspicion from both Church and State.</note>.

Moreover, I have made no mention of the so-frequently occurring phrase, the **curse of nature**; to **remove the curse**—a quite peculiar turn of phrase which these very **theosophical chemists** <note>original: "theosophischen Chymiker"; practitioners of "spiritual alchemy" who believed chemical processes mirrored spiritual transformation.</note>, especially since **Jacob Böhme**, have repeated so very often. It is open to me and all thinking Christians to disapprove of and entirely avoid this whole opinion, hypothesis, and turn of phrase as a mere whim of some interpreters of the first chapters of the **First Book of Moses** <note>The Book of Genesis.</note>; it is an edifying contemplation that is not at all necessary for all Christians. **Paul** <note>The Apostle Paul.</note> is a better judge of the sublime difference of the new spiritual religion, which has not the slightest essential connection with the narrative of **Genesis 1, 2, and 3**; therefore he says so loudly, *every creature of God is good* <note>A reference to 1 Timothy 4:4: "For every creature of God is good, and nothing to be refused..."</note>; he praises the universality of God’s benefits over all people, who should recognize the glory of God from this magnificent **Creation**; sun, rain, and fruitful seasons—there is no curse there, if the people

<summary>The author dismisses the alchemical concept of a "cursed nature" as an unnecessary theological invention, arguing instead for a Pauline view that God's creation remains inherently good.</summary>
<keywords>Freemasons, theosophical chemists, Jacob Böhme, Curse of Nature, Book of Genesis, Apostle Paul, Creation</keywords>

--- Page 208 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s critique of the "chemical theosophists." It follows the argument that the Apostle Paul prioritized the glory of God's creation over the mystical, alchemical interpretations of Genesis favored by the author's opponents.</meta>

<page-num>154</page-num>

...recognize the power and divinity of the Author; just as **Christ** never thought of this petty, childish meditation, which belongs to the upbringing of immature people. These **chemical theosophists** <note>Followers of "spiritual alchemy" like Jacob Böhme, who believed chemical processes were hidden in the Bible.</note> transform the **poor, weak elements** <note>original: "armen, dürftigen Elemente"; a reference to Galatians 4:9, where Paul critiques returning to elementary or ritualistic religious practices.</note>—as Paul calls the **Old Testament**—into secrets, in order to recommend themselves more easily to the simple-minded crowd using this old, **dead letter** <note>A phrase used to describe a literal or superficial reading of scripture that lacks spiritual or rational depth.</note>, claiming that such physical, physiological, and chemical tricks are hidden within it. We are quite happy to leave them to their practice and edification; but we want to guard ourselves diligently so that such "new masters" do not rule over us once more with their theory and endless secrets, bringing us under a **yoke** of old ignorance and blind dependency. We see the merely human manipulation of taking all the books and figures of speech that occur in the **Bible**—written according to the abilities of the people at that time, who could bear no more—and putting them into one oversized lump, context, and connection. This is done with the intention that Christians should continue like children on these **leading-strings** <note>original: "Gängelbande"; straps used to guide toddlers, used here as a metaphor for intellectual or spiritual control.</note>, which are patched together from pieces of the Bible, and should surrender themselves unconditionally to such leaders without the application of their **reason** <note>original: "Verstand"; the faculty of rational understanding.</note>.

<vocab>Christ, Old Testament, chemical, theosophists, Bible, leading-strings, reason</vocab>

<summary>The author warns against "chemical theosophists" who use mystical and alchemical interpretations of the Old Testament to manipulate the simple-minded. He argues that Christians should use their own reason rather than being led by "new masters" who try to return them to a state of childish dependency.</summary>

<keywords>Enlightenment, anti-clericalism, spiritual alchemy, Apostle Paul, religious reason, biblical interpretation, critique of mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 209 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s critique of "chemical theosophists" and their reliance on mystical dogma. The previous page ended with a discussion of how these thinkers use biblical allegories as "leading-strings" to keep Christians in a state of intellectual infancy.</meta>

<page-num>155</page-num>

...pieces. This—precisely this—is the soul of **Papalism** <note>The author uses this term to describe any system of rigid, top-down religious authority and dogmatism, not just the Catholic Church.</note>; it is the death of **moral freedom** and universal love, to which the truly universal Christian religion is intended to elevate all Christians who are capable of it. It is, therefore, a human tradition to continue speaking of the **curse of nature** and to claim possession of **secrets** <note>The "secrets" refer to the hidden knowledge alchemists claimed to possess regarding the spiritual and physical transformation of matter.</note> based on the idea that one must remove the curse from the **metals** — — —. 

All **impurities**—which are only called such by way of comparison—are at the same time completely indispensable for the production of that which we now call the best and the pure. Even the yeast-like or impure parts are thoroughly necessary; they are the husk that covers and protects the kernel; they are, so to speak, the magnet, the tubes through which the relevant nutritional parts are attracted from the air and prepared, etc. For this reason, the author of *The Light of Light* <note>original: "Lichts des Lichts"; a 1763 alchemical work by Johann Ferdinand von Frydau.</note> (Johann Ferdinand von Frydau, 1763, 8vo), on page 126, wrote directly: 

“One errs if one assumes, according to common legend (here called a ‘secret’), that this impurity has its basis in the **Fall of Lucifer and Adam**. They are the most necessary tools of nature,” etc. 

In short, this **mysticism** is nothing more than a new **Scholasticism** <note>Scholasticism was a medieval method of learning that emphasized tradition and dogma; the author views "spiritual alchemy" as a modern, equally restrictive version of this.</note> of a party that thoroughly [achieves] its intentions thereby...

<meta>catchword: achieve [reicht]</meta>

<summary>The author argues that the alchemical concept of a "curse on nature" is a human invention used to justify spiritual hierarchies. He cites the alchemist von Frydau to argue that "impurities" in nature are actually necessary biological or physical tools rather than a result of the biblical Fall.</summary>

<keywords>Papalism, moral freedom, curse of nature, alchemy, Johann Ferdinand von Frydau, Scholasticism, Fall of Man, mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 210 ---
<meta>This page concludes the author’s critique of the "chemical theosophists" and their political-religious ambitions. The previous page discussed how these groups use alchemical "secrets" and spiritual allegories to manipulate their followers.</meta>

<page-num>156</page-num>

...desires to reach out, but does not yet have the courage to state freely and publicly what it dislikes about institutional religion—which it still wants to turn into a **Babel** <note>A biblical reference to the Tower of Babel, used here to signify a state of spiritual confusion, tyranny, or corruption that the author believes these groups seek to impose or maintain.</note>; those who are even less satisfied with the great freedom currently enjoyed by all sensible Christians, who are now subject only to God and their rightful government—not to the Pope, nor to the Church; because, through their **mysticism** and **theosophy**, they are likewise unable to obtain supreme authority over everything. This principle is their secret; they are working toward a **revolution**, which they very childishly call the **Day of the Lord** <note>original: "Tag des Herrn"; a term for divine judgment or the end of the world, here used by the author to mock the eschatological pretensions of these mystical groups.</note>, and with such poor, ancient literalism <note>original: "Buchstaben"; literally "letters," referring here to a rigid or superstitious adherence to old texts used to influence the gullible.</note>, they very easily find an entry point among ignorant and discontented contemporaries.

However, I shall stop writing more to you; so that you may now consider the content of my letters for yourself and your friends, and make such good use of them as circumstances allow.

---

->H a l l e,<-
->printed by **Friedrich Daniel Francke**.<-

<summary>The author concludes his warning against mystical and theosophical sects, accusing them of plotting a "revolution" under the guise of religious prophecy. He contrasts their desire for "supreme authority" with the freedom of the individual Christian in the Enlightenment era.</summary>

<keywords>Babel, religious freedom, mysticism, theosophy, revolution, Day of the Lord, Halle, Friedrich Daniel Francke, Enlightenment critique</keywords>
